<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01QcZE7XLYNrTVohcLyJdaC5`</summary>

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"\\\\documentclass{article}\\n\\\\usepackage{corl_2026}\\n\\\\usepackage{amsmath,amssymb,graphicx,booktabs,xcolor,hyperref,microtype}\\n\\\\usepackage[normalem]{ulem}\\n\\n\\\\newcommand{\\\\placeholder}[1]{\\\\textcolor{red}{[#1]}}\\n\\\\newcommand{\\\\ours}{GeoSafe}\\n\\\\newcommand{\\\\pimodel}{$\\\\pi_{0.5}$}\\n\\n\\\\title{GeoSafe: Internalizing Collision Geometry into Flow-Matching VLA Weights for Zero-Overhead Safe Manipulation}\\n\\n\\\\author{Anonymous}\\n\\n\\\\begin{document}\\n\\\\maketitle\\n\\n\\\\begin{abstract}\\nVision-Language-Action (VLA) models have advanced robot manipulation toward general-purpose deployment, yet collision safety remains an unresolved challenge for real-world use. Most existing safety methods operate at inference time, adding computational overhead; training-time alternatives based on reinforcement learning reduce violations but lack geometric grounding for precise spatial collision reasoning.\\n\\nWe propose \\\\ours{}, a training-time approach that embeds collision awareness directly into flow-matching VLA weights. During fine-tuning, a lightweight auxiliary head predicts per-timestep Signed Distance Field (SDF) values from the model's internal representations, and a collision-shaping loss penalizes trajectories that violate a safety margin. A noise-aware weighting function $w(t)$ suppresses this penalty at high noise levels where geometric predictions are unreliable, activating it only in the low-noise regime where action predictions carry meaningful spatial structure. After training, the SDF head is discarded entirely: the model runs through the standard inference pipeline with zero additional latency, zero additional memory, and no access to scene geometry.\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.45}}"}

Most existing safety methods operate at inference time, adding computational overhead; training-time alternatives based on reinforcement learning reduce violations but lack geometric grounding for precise spatial collision reasoning.\\\\n\\\\nWe propose \\\\\\\\ours{}, a training-time approach that embeds collision awareness directly into flow-matching VLA weights. During fine-tuning, a lightweight auxiliary head predicts per-timestep Signed Distance Field (SDF) values from the model's internal representations, and a collision-shaping loss penalizes trajectories that violate a safety margin. {"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"\\\\subsection{Experimental Setup}\\n\\\\label{sec:setup}\\n%-----------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Base model and training.} We fine-tune $\\\\pi_{0.5}$ (openpi) with full parameter updates (not LoRA) on two NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 GPUs using DDP. The vanilla baseline uses the identical configuration without the SDF head or collision-shaping loss. Full training hyperparameters are in Appendix~\\\\ref{app:training}.\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{\\\\ours{} configuration.} The SDF head is a two-layer MLP (263K parameters) attached to the action expert via a forward hook. Loss weights are $\\\\lambda_{\\\\mathrm{SDF}} = 0.1$ and $\\\\lambda_{\\\\mathrm{col}} = 0.01$ with safety margin $\\\\delta = 2$\\\\,cm and noise-aware gating ($t_0 = 0.5$, $\\\\alpha = 10$). Full configuration is in Appendix~\\\\ref{app:geosafe_config}.\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{SDF ground-truth labels.} Per-timestep signed distance labels are precomputed offline from LIBERO demonstrations using MuJoCo's \\\\texttt{mj\\\\_geomDistance}, covering ${\\\\sim}$75\\\\% of all training tasks (details in Appendix~\\\\ref{app:sdf_labels}).\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Benchmarks.} We evaluate on two benchmarks. SafeLIBERO \\\\citep{hu2025aegis} consists of 32 collision scenarios across four suites that mirror the standard LIBERO \\\\citep{liu2023libero} task categories: SafeLIBERO-Spatial (4 tasks), SafeLIBERO-Object (4 tasks), SafeLIBERO-Goal (4 tasks), and SafeLIBERO-Long (4 tasks with longer horizons). Each task includes two difficulty levels (Level I and Level II), yielding 32 collision scenarios in total. We additionally evaluate on standard LIBERO-Spatial (10 tasks) to verify that collision awareness does not degrade manipulation performance on tasks without explicit collision hazards.\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Metrics.} We report three metrics. Task Success Rate (TSR) measures the fraction of episodes in which the robot completes the instructed task. Collision Avoidance Rate (CAR) measures the fraction of episodes free of any contact with designated obstacle objects. Safety Score (SS) is defined as $\\\\mathrm{SS} = \\\\mathrm{TSR} \\\\times \\\\mathrm{CAR}$ and captures the joint requirement that a policy must both complete tasks and avoid collisions. We target 3 random seeds per configuration; results in this version report seed=1 with 5 eval trials per task.\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Baselines.} We compare against two methods on the SafeLIBERO benchmark. (1)~$\\\\pi_{0.5}$ vanilla: the same base model trained with identical hyperparameters and data but without the SDF head or collision-shaping loss, serving as a controlled ablation of our method's contribution. (2)~AEGIS/VLSA \\\\citep{hu2025aegis}: the current SafeLIBERO state-of-the-art, which applies a plug-and-play Control Barrier Function (CBF) safety layer on top of a trained VLA.\\n\\n%-----------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n\\\\subsection{Main Results}\\n\\\\label{sec:main_results}\\n%-----------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n\\nTable~\\\\ref{tab:main} presents results on SafeLIBERO and standard LIBERO.\\n\\n\\\\begin{table}[t]\\n\\\\caption{Safety Score ($\\\\mathrm{SS} = \\\\mathrm{TSR} \\\\times \\\\mathrm{CAR}$) on SafeLIBERO (4 suites) and Task Success Rate on standard LIBERO-Spatial. Results report seed=1 with 5 eval trials per task. Best results in \\\\textbf{bold}, second-best \\\\uline{underlined}. Per-suite TSR and CAR breakdowns are provided in Appendix Table~\\\\ref{tab:full_results}.}\\n\\\\label{tab:main}\\n\\\\centering\\n\\\\small\\n\\\\begin{tabular}{lcccccc}\\n\\\\toprule\\nMethod & Safe-Spatial & Safe-Object & Safe-Goal & Safe-Long & Avg SS & Std-LIBERO TSR \\\\\\\\\\n\\\\midrule\\n$\\\\pi_{0.5}$ vanilla & \\\\placeholder{P} & \\\\placeholder{P} & \\\\placeholder{P} & \\\\placeholder{P} & \\\\placeholder{P} & \\\\placeholder{P} \\\\\\\\\\nAEGIS/VLSA & \\\\placeholder{P} & \\\\placeholder{P} & \\\\placeholder{P} & \\\\placeholder{P} & \\\\placeholder{P} & \\\\placeholder{P} \\\\\\\\\\n\\\\ours{} (ours) & \\\\placeholder{P} & \\\\placeholder{P} & \\\\placeholder{P} & \\\\placeholder{P} & \\\\placeholder{P} & \\\\placeholder{P} \\\\\\\\\\n\\\\bottomrule\\n\\\\end{tabular}\\n\\\\end{table}\\n\\n\\\\ours{} achieve\\n\\n... [截断: 输出过长，省略 1560 字符] ...\\n\\nar}\\n\\\\end{table}\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{No collision loss} ($\\\\lambda_{\\\\mathrm{col}} = 0$, $\\\\lambda_{\\\\mathrm{SDF}} = 0.1$). Retains SDF regression but removes the collision penalty, testing whether distance prediction alone suffices to improve safety.\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{No backbone gradient} (\\\\texttt{sdf\\\\_grad\\\\_to\\\\_backbone = False}). Detaches the SDF head's input from the computation graph so that SDF gradients train only the auxiliary head without modifying action generation weights.\\n\\n\\\\subsection{Analysis}\\n\\\\label{sec:analysis}\\n%-----------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Training dynamics.} The SDF head's mean absolute error decreases from \\\\placeholder{X.X}\\\\,cm (step 500) to \\\\placeholder{X.X}\\\\,cm (step 30,000). We exclude OmniGuide from Table~\\\\ref{tab:main} because its evaluation uses RoboCasa rather than SafeLIBERO; conceptually, it represents the opposite end of the internalization spectrum (inference-time SDF reasoning vs.\\\\ our weight-level absorption).\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Inference latency.} \\\\ours{} in zero-overhead mode adds no latency. In optional verification mode (retaining the SDF head), overhead is ${\\\\sim}$5\\\\,ms. By contrast, AEGIS adds \\\\placeholder{XX}\\\\,ms and OmniGuide adds \\\\placeholder{XX}\\\\,ms per step.\\n\\n\\n\\n%=============================================================================\\n\\\\section{Conclusion}\\n\\\\label{sec:conclusion}\\n%=============================================================================\\n\\nThis paper presented \\\\ours{}, a training-time method that embeds geometric collision awareness directly into VLA flow-matching weights through SDF auxiliary supervision and a noise-aware collision-shaping loss. Unlike prior approaches that impose safety at inference time through guided sampling \\\\citep{song2026omniguide}, post-hoc trajectory correction \\\\citep{hu2025aegis}, or constrained reinforcement learning \\\\citep{zhang2025safevla}, \\\\ours{} produces a model whose standard forward pass generates inherently collision-aware actions with zero additional latency, memory, or access to scene geometry at deployment.\\n\\nOur results on SafeLIBERO demonstrate \\\\placeholder{XX}\\\\% collision avoidance rate (vs.\\\\ \\\\placeholder{XX}\\\\% for vanilla $\\\\pi_{0.5}$) while maintaining \\\\placeholder{XX}\\\\% task success, validating that SDF collision geometry can be internalized into VLA flow-matching weights through auxiliary supervision and a noise-aware collision-shaping loss, and that the resulting model runs through the unmodified $\\\\pi_{0.5}$ inference pipeline with zero overhead.\\n\\nThe ``train-with, infer-without'' paradigm is not specific to collision avoidance: the same framework could internalize other auxiliary objectives (energy efficiency, human comfort zones) by substituting the SDF head with a corresponding prediction target. Several directions remain open: neural SDF estimation from depth observations for real-world deployment, evaluation on additional VLA backbones (Octo, RT-2), and combining \\\\ours{}'s geometric shaping with constrained RL \\\\citep{zhao2024scpo,zhang2025safevla} for multi-step safety guarantees.\\n\\n\\\\subsection*{Limitations}\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Single benchmark and backbone.} All experiments were conducted on LIBERO/SafeLIBERO with $\\\\pi_{0.5}$. Validating on additional benchmarks (ManiSkill, RoboCasa) and VLA architectures (Octo, RT-2) would strengthen generality claims.\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Known geometry assumption.} Ground-truth SDF labels require analytical scene geometry (MuJoCo). Real-world deployment will need neural SDF estimation from depth or point clouds, and the effect of label noise on collision shaping remains uncharacterized.\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Simulation only.} All results are simulated; a real-robot validation is needed to confirm that internalized collision awareness survives the sim-to-real gap.\\n\\n\\\\subsection*{Broader Impact}\\n\\nA broader impact discussion is provided in Appendix~\\\\ref{app:broader_impact}.\\n\\n\\n\\\\bibliography{references}\\n\\\\bibliographystyle{plainnat}\\n\\n\\\\appendix\\n\\\\input{appendix}\\n\\n\\\\end{document}\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.45}}"}

Per-suite TSR and CAR breakdowns are provided in Appendix Table~\\\\ref{tab:full_results}.}\\n\\\\label{tab:main}\\n\\\\centering\\n\\\\small\\n\\\\begin{tabular}{lcccccc}\\n\\\\toprule\\nMethod & Safe-Spatial & Safe-Object & Safe-Goal & Safe-Long & Avg SS & Std-LIBERO TSR \\\\\\\\\\n\\\\midrule\\n$\\\\pi_{0.5}$ vanilla & \\\\placeholder{P} & \\\\placeholder{P} & \\\\placeholder{P} & \\\\placeholder{P} & \\\\placeholder{P} & \\\\placeholder{P} \\\\\\\\\\nAEGIS/VLSA & \\\\placeholder{P} & \\\\placeholder{P} & \\\\placeholder{P} & \\\\placeholder{P} & \\\\placeholder{P} & \\\\placeholder{P} \\\\\\\\\\n\\\\ours{} (ours) & \\\\placeholder{P} & \\\\placeholder{P} & \\\\placeholder{P} & \\\\placeholder{P} & \\\\placeholder{P} & \\\\placeholder{P} \\\\\\\\\\n\\\\bottomrule\\n\\\\end{tabular}\\n\\\\end{table}\\n\\n\\\\ours{} achieve\\n\\n... [截断: 输出过长，省略 1560 字符] ...\\n\\nar}\\n\\\\end{table}\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{No collision loss} ($\\\\lambda_{\\\\mathrm{col}} = 0$, $\\\\lambda_{\\\\mathrm{SDF}} = 0.1$). Retains SDF regression but removes the collision penalty, testing whether distance prediction alone suffices to improve safety.\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{No backbone gradient} (\\\\texttt{sdf\\\\_grad\\\\_to\\\\_backbone = False}). Detaches the SDF head's input from the computation graph so that SDF gradients train only the auxiliary head without modifying action generation weights.\\n\\n\\\\subsection{Analysis}\\n\\\\label{sec:analysis}\\n%-----------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Training dynamics.} The SDF head's mean absolute error decreases from \\\\placeholder{X.X}\\\\,cm (step 500) to \\\\placeholder{X.X}\\\\,cm (step 30,000). We exclude OmniGuide from Table~\\\\ref{tab:main} because its evaluation uses RoboCasa rather than SafeLIBERO; conceptually, it represents the opposite end of the internalization spectrum (inference-time SDF reasoning vs.\\\\ our weight-level absorption).\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Inference latency.} \\\\ours{} in zero-overhead mode adds no latency. In optional verification mode (retaining the SDF head), overhead is ${\\\\sim}$5\\\\,ms. By contrast, AEGIS adds \\\\placeholder{XX}\\\\,ms and OmniGuide adds \\\\placeholder{XX}\\\\,ms per step.\\n\\n\\n\\n%=============================================================================\\n\\\\section{Conclusion}\\n\\\\label{sec:conclusion}\\n%=============================================================================\\n\\nThis paper presented \\\\ours{}, a training-time method that embeds geometric collision awareness directly into VLA flow-matching weights through SDF auxiliary supervision and a noise-aware collision-shaping loss. Unlike prior approaches that impose safety at inference time through guided sampling \\\\citep{song2026omniguide}, post-hoc trajectory correction \\\\citep{hu2025aegis}, or constrained reinforcement learning \\\\citep{zhang2025safevla}, \\\\ours{} produces a model whose standard forward pass generates inherently collision-aware actions with zero additional latency, memory, or access to scene geometry at deployment.\\n\\nOur results on SafeLIBERO demonstrate \\\\placeholder{XX}\\\\% collision avoidance rate (vs.\\\\ \\\\placeholder{XX}\\\\% for vanilla $\\\\pi_{0.5}$) while maintaining \\\\placeholder{XX}\\\\% task success, validating that SDF collision geometry can be internalized into VLA flow-matching weights through auxiliary supervision and a noise-aware collision-shaping loss, and that the resulting model runs through the unmodified $\\\\pi_{0.5}$ inference pipeline with zero overhead.\\n\\nThe ``train-with, infer-without'' paradigm is not specific to collision avoidance: the same framework could internalize other auxiliary objectives (energy efficiency, human comfort zones) by substituting the SDF head with a corresponding prediction target. Several directions remain open: neural SDF estimation from depth observations for real-world deployment, evaluation on additional VLA backbones (Octo, RT-2), and combining \\\\ours{}'s geometric shaping with constrained RL \\\\citep{zhao2024scpo,zhang2025safevla} for multi-step safety guarantees.\\n\\n\\\\subsection*{Limitations}\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Single benchmark and backbone.} All experiments were conducted on LIBERO/SafeLIBERO with $\\\\pi_{0.5}$. Validating on additional benchmarks (ManiSkill, RoboCasa) and VLA architectures (Octo, RT-2) would strengthen generality claims.\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Known geometry assumption.} Ground-truth SDF labels require analytical scene geometry (MuJoCo). Real-world deployment will need neural SDF estimation from depth or point clouds, and the effect of label noise on collision shaping remains uncharacterized.\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Simulation only.} All results are simulated; a real-robot validation is needed to confirm that internalized collision awareness survives the sim-to-real gap.\\n\\n\\\\subsection*{Broader Impact}\\n\\nA broader impact discussion is provided in Appendix~\\\\ref{app:broader_impact}.\\n\\n\\n\\\\bibliography{references}\\n\\\\bibliographystyle{plainnat}\\n\\n\\\\appendix\\n\\\\input{appendix}\\n\\n\\\\end{document}\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.45}}"

Most existing safety methods operate at inference time, adding computational overhead; training-time alternatives based on reinforcement learning reduce violations but lack geometric grounding for precise spatial collision reasoning.\\n\\nWe propose \\\\ours{}, a training-time approach that embeds collision awareness directly into flow-matching VLA weights. During fine-tuning, a lightweight auxiliary head predicts per-timestep Signed Distance Field (SDF) values from the model's internal representations, and a collision-shaping loss penalizes trajectories that violate a safety margin. A noise-aware weighting function $w(t)$ suppresses this penalty at high noise levels where geometric predictions are unreliable, activating it only in the low-noise regime where action predictions carry meaningful spatial structure. After training, the SDF head is discarded entirely: the model runs through the standard inference pipeline with zero additional latency, zero additional memory, and no access to scene geometry.\\n\\nWe evaluate \\\\ours{} on the SafeLIBERO benchmark (32 collision scenarios across 4 task suites), measuring Task Success Rate (TSR) and Collision Avoidance Rate (CAR). \\\\ours{} achieves \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% TSR and \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% CAR, compared to \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% TSR / \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% CAR for vanilla $\\\\pi_{0.5}$ and \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% TSR / \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% CAR for the strongest inference-time baseline. Our approach establishes a connection between guidance distillation in image generation and safety internalization in robot control, providing a ``train-with, infer-without'' framework for embedding geometric safety constraints into generative action policies.\\n\\\\end{abstract}\\n\\n%=============================================================================\\n\\\\section{Introduction}\\n%=============================================================================\\n\\nVision-Language-Action (VLA) models have emerged as a powerful paradigm for general-purpose robot manipulation. $\\\\pi_0$ \\\\citep{black2024pi0} demonstrated that a flow-matching architecture built on a pretrained vision-language model can master diverse manipulation tasks across multiple robot platforms, and its successor $\\\\pi_{0.5}$ \\\\citep{black2025pi05} extended this capability to open-world generalization through co-training on heterogeneous data. These models generate smooth, language-conditioned action trajectories by learning velocity fields that transport noise distributions to action distributions, enabling flexible deployment without task-specific engineering. Yet a critical gap separates laboratory success from real-world deployment: collision safety. When a robot executes a manipulation task in a cluttered environment, unintended contact with surrounding objects or the workspace boundary can damage both the robot and the environment, and create safety hazards for nearby humans. The problem is especially acute for VLA models, which generate entire action chunks in a single forward pass without explicit geometric reasoning about obstacle proximity.\\n\\nExisting approaches to VLA collision safety fall into two categories. Inference-time methods---OmniGuide \\\\citep{song2026omniguide} (SDF-guided denoising, ${\\\\sim}2\\\\times$ latency overhead), AEGIS/VLSA \\\\citep{hu2025aegis} (CBF trajectory projection), SafeDiffuser \\\\citep{xiao2025safediffuser} (CBF-embedded diffusion)---add computational overhead and risk pushing corrected trajectories into out-of-distribution states where subsequent predictions degrade. Training-time methods such as SafeVLA \\\\citep{zhang2025safevla} reduce violations through constrained RL but rely on abstract cost functions without geometric grounding. A fundamental tension persists: inference-time interventions add latency and risk OOD drift, while training-time RL methods lack geometric structure to reason about collision boundaries.\\n\\nWe draw on an observation from image generation: guidance distillation \\\\citep{meng2023distillation} has shown that inference-time guidance signals can be internalized into model weights during training. We transfer this principle to VLA safety, internalizing SDF collision geometry into flow-matching velocity fields for safe action generation. At low noise levels ($t$ near 0), the model's internal representations encode meaningful geometric structure; an SDF auxiliary head attached to these representations provides a differentiable collision proximity signal. By penalizing collision-prone predictions with a noise-dependent weight that activates only in the low-noise regime, we shape the learned velocity field to route around obstacles without corrupting the training signal at high noise levels.\\n\\nWe propose \\\\ours{}, a training-time method that embeds collision awareness into $\\\\pi_{0.5}$ through SDF auxiliary supervision and a noise-aware collision-shaping loss. A lightweight two-layer network predicts per-timestep minimum signed distances from the action expert's internal features. The collision-shaping loss penalizes trajectories violating a safety margin $\\\\delta$, weighted by $w(t) = \\\\sigma(\\\\alpha(t_0 - t))$ that activates only in the low-noise regime. Both SDF regression and collision penalty gradients propagate through the shared backbone, directly modifying the velocity field. At inference, the SDF head is discarded entirely: the model runs through the standard $\\\\pi_{0.5}$ pipeline with zero additional latency, memory, or access to scene geometry.\\n\\nOur contributions are as follows:\\n\\\\begin{itemize}\\n\\\\item We present the first method that internalizes SDF collision geometry into VLA flow-matching weights during training, producing a model that generates inherently collision-aware actions with zero inference overhead. The SDF auxiliary head is dropped at deployment, and the stand\\n\\n... [截断: 输出过长，省略 20248 字符] ...\\n\\nar}\\n\\\\end{table}\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{No collision loss} ($\\\\lambda_{\\\\mathrm{col}} = 0$, $\\\\lambda_{\\\\mathrm{SDF}} = 0.1$). Retains SDF regression but removes the collision penalty, testing whether distance prediction alone suffices to improve safety.\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{No backbone gradient} (\\\\texttt{sdf\\\\_grad\\\\_to\\\\_backbone = False}). Detaches the SDF head's input from the computation graph so that SDF gradients train only the auxiliary head without modifying action generation weights.\\n\\n\\\\subsection{Analysis}\\n\\\\label{sec:analysis}\\n%-----------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Training dynamics.} The SDF head's mean absolute error decreases from \\\\placeholder{X.X}\\\\,cm (step 500) to \\\\placeholder{X.X}\\\\,cm (step 30,000). We exclude OmniGuide from Table~\\\\ref{tab:main} because its evaluation uses RoboCasa rather than SafeLIBERO; conceptually, it represents the opposite end of the internalization spectrum (inference-time SDF reasoning vs.\\\\ our weight-level absorption).\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Inference latency.} \\\\ours{} in zero-overhead mode adds no latency. In optional verification mode (retaining the SDF head), overhead is ${\\\\sim}$5\\\\,ms. By contrast, AEGIS adds \\\\placeholder{XX}\\\\,ms and OmniGuide adds \\\\placeholder{XX}\\\\,ms per step.\\n\\n\\n\\n%=============================================================================\\n\\\\section{Conclusion}\\n\\\\label{sec:conclusion}\\n%=============================================================================\\n\\nThis paper presented \\\\ours{}, a training-time method that embeds geometric collision awareness directly into VLA flow-matching weights through SDF auxiliary supervision and a noise-aware collision-shaping loss. Unlike prior approaches that impose safety at inference time through guided sampling \\\\citep{song2026omniguide}, post-hoc trajectory correction \\\\citep{hu2025aegis}, or constrained reinforcement learning \\\\citep{zhang2025safevla}, \\\\ours{} produces a model whose standard forward pass generates inherently collision-aware actions with zero additional latency, memory, or access to scene geometry at deployment.\\n\\nOur results on SafeLIBERO demonstrate \\\\placeholder{XX}\\\\% collision avoidance rate (vs.\\\\ \\\\placeholder{XX}\\\\% for vanilla $\\\\pi_{0.5}$) while maintaining \\\\placeholder{XX}\\\\% task success, validating that SDF collision geometry can be internalized into VLA flow-matching weights through auxiliary supervision and a noise-aware collision-shaping loss, and that the resulting model runs through the unmodified $\\\\pi_{0.5}$ inference pipeline with zero overhead.\\n\\nThe ``train-with, infer-without'' paradigm is not specific to collision avoidance: the same framework could internalize other auxiliary objectives (energy efficiency, human comfort zones) by substituting the SDF head with a corresponding prediction target. Several directions remain open: neural SDF estimation from depth observations for real-world deployment, evaluation on additional VLA backbones (Octo, RT-2), and combining \\\\ours{}'s geometric shaping with constrained RL \\\\citep{zhao2024scpo,zhang2025safevla} for multi-step safety guarantees.\\n\\n\\\\subsection*{Limitations}\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Single benchmark and backbone.} All experiments were conducted on LIBERO/SafeLIBERO with $\\\\pi_{0.5}$. Validating on additional benchmarks (ManiSkill, RoboCasa) and VLA architectures (Octo, RT-2) would strengthen generality claims.\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Known geometry assumption.} Ground-truth SDF labels require analytical scene geometry (MuJoCo). Real-world deployment will need neural SDF estimation from depth or point clouds, and the effect of label noise on collision shaping remains uncharacterized.\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Simulation only.} All results are simulated; a real-robot validation is needed to confirm that internalized collision awareness survives the sim-to-real gap.\\n\\n\\\\subsection*{Broader Impact}\\n\\nA broader impact discussion is provided in Appendix~\\\\ref{app:broader_impact}.\\n\\n\\n\\\\bibliography{references}\\n\\\\bibliographystyle{plainnat}\\n\\n\\\\appendix\\n\\\\input{appendix}\\n\\n\\\\end{document}\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.5}}"}

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   I am the independent scientific reviewer for the `geosafe_vla` project (GeoSafe: Internalizing Geometric Collision Awareness into VLA Weights via SDF collision-shaping loss). My role is to independently audit experiment conclusions, verify design decisions, check GPU boundary compliance (L1 red line), review paper drafts (mode B: adversarial writing review), and post findings to the Director via `reviewer_post_to_director`. All communication must be in Chinese. The project targets **CoRL 2026** (Abstract: 2026-05-26, Paper: 2026-05-29). Server: westd-18751 (root).

   In this session, I performed: (a) holistic full-paper review across 7 draft sections, (b) ROADMAP reviews, (c) training progress audits, (d) critical eval pipeline bug investigation, (e) strategic analysis on claims/ablation/timeline, (f) LaTeX compilation and page-limit review, (g) compression quality review. Most recently, I was asked to do a **final structural review of the compressed 8-page LaTeX PDF** before the training space window ends.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **GeoSafe**: Internalizes SDF collision geometry into π₀.₅ VLA weights during flow-matching training via collision-shaping loss. SDF head dropped at inference for zero overhead.
   - **π₀.₅ / OpenPi**: Flow-matching VLA base model, PyTorch training via `scripts/train_geosafe.py` and `scripts/train_pytorch.py`
   - **Flow Matching Convention**: `x_t = (1-t)*a + t*ε`, t=0 clean, t=1 pure noise
   - **Collision-Shaping Loss**: L = L_FM + λ_sdf * L_SDF + λ_col * L_col
   - **w(t) = σ(α(t₀ - t))**: Sigmoid gating, t₀=0.5, α=10. Suppresses collision penalty at high noise.
   - **SDF Head**: Two-layer MLP Linear(1024,256)→ReLU→Linear(256,1), 262,657 params, attached via forward hook on `action_out_proj`
   - **D005 Config**: 2-GPU DDP per run (baseline GPU 0,1 + GeoSafe GPU 2,3 parallel), batch_size=128 (64/GPU), 30K steps, warmup=10,000, save_interval=5000
   - **Training hyperparams**: AdamW, peak_lr=5e-5, cosine decay, max_norm=1.0, λ_SDF=0.1, λ_col=0.01, δ=0.02m, bfloat16 precision
   - **SDF label coverage**: 57 tasks / 8 suites / ~75% of training tasks
   - **SafeLIBERO**: 4 suites × 4 tasks × 2 difficulty levels (I/II) = 32 collision scenarios
   - **Eval pipeline bug (FIXED)**: Image rotation was `[::-1]` (vertical flip only) instead of `[::-1, ::-1]` (180° rotation). Fixed but TSR still 0% at 5K steps due to insufficient training (640K vs 7.68M samples).
   - **Training insufficiency root cause**: Official openpi uses bs=256 × 30K steps = 7.68M samples; D004 used bs=128 × 5K = 640K (12× gap). D005 uses bs=128 × 30K = 3.84M (50% of official).
   - **Competitors**: AEGIS/VLSA (CBF post-hoc), OmniGuide (inference-time SDF, ~2× latency), SafeVLA (constrained RL), SafeDiffuser (CBF in diffusion), SCPO (state-wise constrained policy optimization)
   - **Minimum viable paper**: 1 seed + 2 ablations (A1: no collision loss, A2: no backbone gradient), Table 1 (3 methods) + Table 2 (3 rows)
   - **CoRL 2026**: 8 pages main + unlimited refs/appendix

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"     1\\t\\\\documentclass{article}\\n     2\\t\\\\usepackage{corl_2026}\\n     3\\t\\\\usepackage{amsmath,amssymb,graphicx,booktabs,xcolor,hyperref,microtype}\\n     4\\t\\\\usepackage[normalem]{ulem}\\n     5\\t\\n     6\\t\\\\newcommand{\\\\placeholder}[1]{\\\\textcolor{red}{[#1]}}\\n     7\\t\\\\newcommand{\\\\ours}{GeoSafe}\\n     8\\t\\\\newcommand{\\\\pimodel}{$\\\\pi_{0.5}$}\\n     9\\t\\n    10\\t\\\\title{GeoSafe: Internalizing Collision Geometry into Flow-Matching VLA Weights for Zero-Overhead Safe Manipulation}\\n    11\\t\\n    12\\t\\\\author{Anonymous}\\n    13\\t\\n    14\\t\\\\begin{document}\\n    15\\t\\\\maketitle\\n    16\\t\\n    17\\t\\\\begin{abstract}\\n    18\\tVision-Language-Action (VLA) models have advanced robot manipulation toward general-purpose deployment, yet collision safety remains an unresolved challenge for real-world use. Most existing safety methods operate at inference time, adding computational overhead; training-time alternatives based on reinforcement learning reduce violations but lack geometric grounding for precise spatial collision reasoning.\\n    19\\t\\n    20\\tWe propose \\\\ours{}, a training-time approach that embeds collision awareness directly into flow-matching VLA weights. During fine-tuning, a lightweight auxiliary head predicts per-timestep Signed Distance Field (SDF) values from the model's internal representations, and a collision-shaping loss penalizes trajectories that violate a safety margin. A noise-aware weighting function $w(t)$ suppresses this penalty at high noise levels where geometric predictions are unreliable, activating it only in the low-noise regime where action predictions carry meaningful spatial structure. After training, the SDF head is discarded entirely: the model runs through the standard inference pipeline with zero additional latency, zero additional memory, and no access to scene geometry.\\n    21\\t\\n    22\\tWe evaluate \\\\ours{} on the SafeLIBERO benchmark (32 collision scenarios across 4 task suites), measuring Task Success Rate (TSR) and Collision Avoidance Rate (CAR). \\\\ours{} achieves \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% TSR and \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% CAR, compared to \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% TSR / \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% CAR for vanilla $\\\\pi_{0.5}$ and \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% TSR / \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% CAR for the strongest inference-time baseline. Our approach establishes a connection between guidance distillation in image generation and safety internalization in robot control, providing a ``train-with, infer-without'' framework for embedding geometric safety constraints into generative action policies.\\n    23\\t\\\\end{abstract}\\n    24\\t\\n    25\\t%=============================================================================\\n    26\\t\\\\section{Introduction}\\n    27\\t%=============================================================================\\n    28\\t\\n    29\\tVision-Language-Action (VLA) models have emerged as a powerful paradigm for general-purpose robot manipulation. $\\\\pi_0$ \\\\citep{black2024pi0} demonstrated that a flow-matching architecture built on a pretrained vision-language model can master diverse manipulation tasks across multiple robot platforms, and its successor $\\\\pi_{0.5}$ \\\\citep{black2025pi05} extended this capability to open-world generalization through co-training on heterogeneous data. These models generate smooth, language-conditioned action trajectories by learning velocity fields that transport noise distributions to action distributions, enabling flexible deployment without task-specific engineering. Yet a critical gap separates laboratory success from real-world deployment: collision safety. When a robot executes a manipulation task in a cluttered environment, unintended contact with surrounding objects or the workspace boundary can damage both the robot and the environment, and create safety hazards for nearby humans. The problem is especially acute for VLA models, which generate entire action chunks in a single forward pass without explicit geometric reasoning about obstacle proximity.\\n    30\\t\\n    31\\tExisting approaches to VLA collision safety fall into two categories. Unlike prior approaches that impose safety at inference time through guided sampling \\\\citep{song2026omniguide}, post-hoc trajectory correction \\\\citep{hu2025aegis}, or constrained reinforcement learning \\\\citep{zhang2025safevla}, \\\\ours{} produces a model whose standard forward pass generates inherently collision-aware actions with zero additional latency, memory, or access to scene geometry at deployment.\\n   253\\t\\n   254\\tOur results on SafeLIBERO demonstrate \\\\placeholder{XX}\\\\% collision avoidance rate (vs.\\\\ \\\\placeholder{XX}\\\\% for vanilla $\\\\pi_{0.5}$) while maintaining \\\\placeholder{XX}\\\\% task success, validating that SDF collision geometry can be internalized into VLA flow-matching weights through auxiliary supervision and a noise-aware collision-shaping loss, and that the resulting model runs through the unmodified $\\\\pi_{0.5}$ inference pipeline with zero overhead.\\n   255\\t\\n   256\\tThe ``train-with, infer-without'' paradigm is not specific to collision avoidance: the same framework could internalize other auxiliary objectives (energy efficiency, human comfort zones) by substituting the SDF head with a corresponding prediction target. Several directions remain open: neural SDF estimation from depth observations for real-world deployment, evaluation on additional VLA backbones (Octo, RT-2), and combining \\\\ours{}'s geometric shaping with constrained RL \\\\citep{zhao2024scpo,zhang2025safevla} for multi-step safety guarantees.\\n   257\\t\\n   258\\t\\\\subsection*{Limitations}\\n   259\\t\\n   260\\t\\\\paragraph{Single benchmark and backbone.} All experiments were conducted on LIBERO/SafeLIBERO with $\\\\pi_{0.5}$. We enable gradient flow from the SDF head through the shared features $f_t$ into the action expert weights: the feature tensor captured by the forward hook is used without detachment, so that $\\\\nabla_{f_t} \\\\mathcal{L}_{\\\\mathrm{SDF}}$ and $\\\\nabla_{f_t} \\\\mathcal{L}_{\\\\mathrm{col}}$ both contribute to the backbone parameter update.\\n    55\\t\\n    56\\t\\\\subsection{\\\\ours{}-Specific Configuration}\\n    57\\t\\\\label{app:geosafe_config}\\n    58\\t\\n    59\\t\\\\begin{table}[h]\\n    60\\t\\\\centering\\n    61\\t\\\\small\\n    62\\t\\\\caption{\\\\ours{} auxiliary head and loss hyperparameters.}\\n    63\\t\\\\begin{tabular}{ll}\\n    64\\t\\\\toprule\\n    65\\tHyperparameter & Value \\\\\\\\\\n    66\\t\\\\midrule\\n    67\\tSDF head architecture & Linear(1024, 256) $\\\\to$ ReLU $\\\\to$ Linear(256, 1) \\\\\\\\\\n    68\\tSDF head parameters & 262,657 \\\\\\\\\\n    69\\tSDF loss weight ($\\\\lambda_{\\\\mathrm{SDF}}$) & 0.1 \\\\\\\\\\n    70\\tCollision loss weight ($\\\\lambda_{\\\\mathrm{col}}$) & 0.01 \\\\\\\\\\n   \\n\\n... {"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"\\\\documentclass{article}\\n\\\\usepackage{corl_2026}\\n\\\\usepackage{amsmath,amssymb,graphicx,booktabs,xcolor,hyperref,microtype}\\n\\\\usepackage[normalem]{ulem}\\n\\n\\\\newcommand{\\\\placeholder}[1]{\\\\textcolor{red}{[#1]}}\\n\\\\newcommand{\\\\ours}{GeoSafe}\\n\\\\newcommand{\\\\pimodel}{$\\\\pi_{0.5}$}\\n\\n\\\\title{GeoSafe: Internalizing Collision Geometry into Flow-Matching VLA Weights for Zero-Overhead Safe Manipulation}\\n\\n\\\\author{Anonymous}\\n\\n\\\\begin{document}\\n\\\\maketitle\\n\\n\\\\begin{abstract}\\nVision-Language-Action (VLA) models have advanced robot manipulation toward general-purpose deployment, yet collision safety remains an unresolved challenge for real-world use. Most existing safety methods operate at inference time, adding computational overhead; training-time alternatives based on reinforcement learning reduce violations but lack geometric grounding for precise spatial collision reasoning.\\n\\nWe propose \\\\ours{}, a training-time approach that embeds collision awareness directly into flow-matching VLA weights. During fine-tuning, a lightweight auxiliary head predicts per-timestep Signed Distance Field (SDF) values from the model's internal representations, and a collision-shaping loss penalizes trajectories that violate a safety margin. A noise-aware weighting function $w(t)$ suppresses this penalty at high noise levels where geometric predictions are unreliable, activating it only in the low-noise regime where action predictions carry meaningful spatial structure. After training, the SDF head is discarded entirely: the model runs through the standard inference pipeline with zero additional latency, zero additional memory, and no access to scene geometry.\\n\\nWe evaluate \\\\ours{} on the SafeLIBERO benchmark (32 collision scenarios across 4 task suites), measuring Task Success Rate (TSR) and Collision Avoidance Rate (CAR). \\\\ours{} achieves \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% TSR and \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% CAR, compared to \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% TSR / \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% CAR for vanilla $\\\\pi_{0.5}$ and \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% TSR / \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% CAR for the strongest inference-time baseline. Our approach establishes a connection between guidance distillation in image generation and safety internalization in robot control, providing a ``train-with, infer-without'' framework for embedding geometric safety constraints into generative action policies.\\n\\\\end{abstract}\\n\\n%=============================================================================\\n\\\\section{Introduction}\\n%=============================================================================\\n\\nVision-Language-Action (VLA) models have emerged as a powerful paradigm for general-purpose robot manipulation. $\\\\pi_0$ \\\\citep{black2024pi0} demonstrated that a flow-matching architecture built on a pretrained vision-language model can master diverse manipulation tasks across multiple robot platforms, and its successor $\\\\pi_{0.5}$ \\\\citep{black2025pi05} extended this capability to open-world generalization through co-training on heterogeneous data. These models generate smooth, language-conditioned action trajectories by learning velocity fields that transport noise distributions to action distributions, enabling flexible deployment without task-specific engineering. Yet a critical gap separates laboratory success from real-world deployment: collision safety. When a robot executes a manipulation task in a cluttered environment, unintended contact with surrounding objects or the workspace boundary can damage both the robot and the environment, and create safety hazards for nearby humans. The problem is especially acute for VLA models, which generate entire action chunks in a single forward pass without explicit geometric reasoning about obstacle proximity.\\n\\nExisting approaches to VLA collision safety fall into two categories. Inference-time methods---OmniGuide \\\\citep{song2026omniguide} (SDF-guided denoising, ${\\\\sim}2\\\\times$ latency overhead), AEGIS/VLSA \\\\citep{hu2025aegis} (CBF trajectory projection), SafeDiffuser \\\\citep{xiao2025safediffuser} (CBF-embedded \\n\\n... [截断: 输出过长，省略 3433 字符] ...\\n\\nted Work}\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Safe Robot Learning.}\\nInference-time VLA safety methods include SDF-guided denoising \\\\citep{song2026omniguide}, CBF-based trajectory projection \\\\citep{hu2025aegis}, and CBF-embedded diffusion \\\\citep{xiao2025safediffuser}. These approaches offer robustness to novel obstacles through real-time geometric checks, but add latency and risk pushing corrected actions into out-of-distribution states. Training-time approaches such as SafeVLA \\\\citep{zhang2025safevla} reduce safety cost via constrained policy optimization \\\\citep{zhao2024scpo} but lack geometric grounding for precise spatial collision reasoning. \\\\ours{} resolves this tension by internalizing SDF collision geometry into VLA weights during training, producing safe actions with zero inference overhead.\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Vision-Language-Action Models.}\\n$\\\\pi_0$ \\\\citep{black2024pi0} introduced a flow-matching VLA built on PaliGemma, and $\\\\pi_{0.5}$ \\\\citep{black2025pi05} extended it to open-world generalization with open-source weights via the openpi codebase. Recent work has explored geometric priors: GLaD \\\\citep{guo2025glad} distills 3D geometric features into VLA hidden states, achieving 94.1\\\\% success on LIBERO, but targets general spatial understanding rather than collision safety. \\\\ours{} builds on $\\\\pi_{0.5}$ and extends geometric internalization specifically to collision awareness.\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{SDF in Robotics and Guidance Distillation.}\\nSDF representations have been adopted in manipulation planning \\\\citep{chen2026cssdf,weng2023ngdf}, and several VLA works use a ``train-with-geometry, infer-without'' paradigm for general spatial accuracy \\\\citep{liu2025occvla,li2025spatial,qian2025geopredict}. Separately, \\\\citet{meng2023distillation} showed that classifier-free guidance can be distilled into diffusion model weights. \\\\ours{} transfers both ideas to VLA safety: we internalize configuration-space SDF into flow-matching velocity fields through an auxiliary head discarded at inference, combining collision-specific geometric supervision with full weight internalization.\\n\\n\\n\\\\section{Method}\\n\\\\label{sec:method}\\n%=============================================================================\\n\\nWe present \\\\ours{}, a training-time approach that embeds geometric safety awareness into a flow-matching VLA without incurring any additional cost at inference. A lightweight auxiliary head predicts SDF values from the model's internal representations, and a noise-aware collision-shaping loss penalizes trajectories entering collision regions. After training, the auxiliary head is discarded and the base $\\\\pi_{0.5}$ policy is deployed unchanged.\\n\\n\\n\\\\begin{figure*}[t]\\n\\\\centering\\n\\\\includegraphics[width=\\\\textwidth]{figures/fig1_overview.pdf}\\n\\\\caption{\\\\ours{} overview. \\\\textbf{Left:} During training, an SDF head predicts signed distances from the action expert's features. The collision-shaping loss, gated by noise-aware $w(t)$, penalizes trajectories violating safety margin $\\\\delta$. \\\\textbf{Right:} The SDF head is discarded at inference; the model runs through the standard \\\\pimodel{} pipeline with zero overhead.}\\n\\\\label{fig:overview}\\n\\\\end{figure*}\\n\\n%-----------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n\\\\subsection{Problem Formulation}\\n\\\\label{sec:formulation}\\n%-----------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Flow-matching action generation.} $\\\\pi_{0.5}$ generates robot action trajectories through conditional flow matching. Let $a \\\\in \\\\mathbb{R}^{H \\\\times D_a}$ denote a ground-truth action chunk of horizon $H$ and dimension $D_a$, and let $\\\\epsilon \\\\sim \\\\mathcal{N}(0, I)$ be Gaussian noise. The noisy interpolant at time $t \\\\in [0, 1]$ is defined as\\n\\\\begin{equation}\\nx_t = (1 - t) \\\\, a + t \\\\, \\\\epsilon,\\n\\\\end{equation}\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.51}}"}

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"100:\\\\paragraph{Ground-truth SDF computation.} Per-timestep signed distances are precomputed offline using MuJoCo's \\\\texttt{mj\\\\_geomDistance}, measuring minimum distance between end-effector geometries and scene obstacles (excluding grasped objects). Labels are stored as compressed arrays and aligned to action chunks at training time. Implementation details are in Appendix~\\\\ref{app:sdf_labels}.\\n147:\\\\ours{} follows the standard $\\\\pi_{0.5}$ fine-tuning pipeline. We perform full fine-tuning (not LoRA) with AdamW and DDP across two GPUs; complete training hyperparameters are provided in Appendix~\\\\ref{app:training}. Gradients from both SDF losses propagate through the shared backbone features into the action expert weights (not detached), which is the mechanism by which safety awareness becomes embedded in the velocity field. SDF labels cover ${\\\\sim}$75\\\\% of all training tasks; samples without labels are excluded from SDF loss computation.\\n158:Our approach bears a structural analogy to guidance distillation \\\\citep{meng2023distillation}: the SDF head acts as a ``geometric safety classifier'' providing training-time gradients, and after training the model has internalized this signal, making the head dispensable. A detailed comparison is in Appendix~\\\\ref{app:distillation}.\\n173:\\\\paragraph{Base model and training.} We fine-tune $\\\\pi_{0.5}$ (openpi) with full parameter updates (not LoRA) on two NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 GPUs using DDP. The vanilla baseline uses the identical configuration without the SDF head or collision-shaping loss. Full training hyperparameters are in Appendix~\\\\ref{app:training}.\\n175:\\\\paragraph{\\\\ours{} configuration.} The SDF head is a two-layer MLP (263K parameters) attached to the action expert via a forward hook. Loss weights are $\\\\lambda_{\\\\mathrm{SDF}} = 0.1$ and $\\\\lambda_{\\\\mathrm{col}} = 0.01$ with safety margin $\\\\delta = 2$\\\\,cm and noise-aware gating ($t_0 = 0.5$, $\\\\alpha = 10$). Full configuration is in Appendix~\\\\ref{app:geosafe_config}.\\n177:\\\\paragraph{SDF ground-truth labels.} Per-timestep signed distance labels are precomputed offline from LIBERO demonstrations using MuJoCo's \\\\texttt{mj\\\\_geomDistance}, covering ${\\\\sim}$75\\\\% of all training tasks (details in Appendix~\\\\ref{app:sdf_labels}).\\n268:A broader impact discussion is provided in Appendix~\\\\ref{app:broader_impact}.\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.44}}"}
```

We attach the SDF head to these features via a forward hook on the output projection layer, requiring no modification to the base model's source code. The head is defined as\\n\\\\begin{equation}\\n\\\\hat{s} = W_2 \\\\, \\\\mathrm{ReLU}(W_1 \\\\, f_t + b_1) + b_2,\\n\\\\end{equation}\\nwhere $W_1 \\\\in \\\\mathbb{R}^{256 \\\\times 1024}$, $W_2 \\\\in \\\\mathbb{R}^{1 \\\\times 256}$, and $\\\\hat{s} \\\\in \\\\mathbb{R}^{B \\\\times H}$ gives the predicted minimum signed distance for each timestep in the action chunk. The head contains approximately 263K parameters, which is negligible compared to the 300M-parameter action expert.\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Ground-truth SDF computation.} Per-timestep signed distances are precomputed offline using MuJoCo's \\\\texttt{mj\\\\_geomDistance}, measuring minimum distance between end-effector geometries and scene obstacles (excluding grasped objects). Labels are stored as compressed arrays and aligned to action chunks at training time. Implementation details are in Appendix~\\\\ref{app:sdf_labels}.\\n\\n%-----------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n\\\\subsection{Noise-Aware Collision-Shaping Loss}\\n\\\\label{sec:loss}\\n%-----------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n\\nA direct collision penalty on the SDF predictions would be problematic in the flow-matching setting: at high noise levels (large $t$), the noisy actions $x_t$ bear little resemblance to the clean trajectory, and the model's internal features primarily reflect noise rather than meaningful geometric structure. Penalizing collisions uniformly across all noise levels would inject noisy gradients that destabilize training. We address this with a noise-aware weighting scheme that activates the collision penalty only when the model operates on near-clean inputs.\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Noise-aware weight.} We define a time-dependent weight\\n\\\\begin{equation}\\nw(t) = \\\\sigma\\\\!\\\\left(\\\\alpha \\\\, (t_0 - t)\\\\right),\\n\\\\end{equation}\\nwhere $\\\\sigma$ is the sigmoid function, $t_0 = 0.5$ is the transition center, and $\\\\alpha = 10$ controls the sharpness. When $t \\\\ll t_0$ (low noise), $w(t\\n\\n... [截断: 输出过长，省略 11248 字符] ...\\n\\nar}\\n\\\\end{table}\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{No collision loss} ($\\\\lambda_{\\\\mathrm{col}} = 0$, $\\\\lambda_{\\\\mathrm{SDF}} = 0.1$). Retains SDF regression but removes the collision penalty, testing whether distance prediction alone suffices to improve safety.\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{No backbone gradient} (\\\\texttt{sdf\\\\_grad\\\\_to\\\\_backbone = False}). Detaches the SDF head's input from the computation graph so that SDF gradients train only the auxiliary head without modifying action generation weights.\\n\\n\\\\subsection{Analysis}\\n\\\\label{sec:analysis}\\n%-----------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Training dynamics.} The SDF head's mean absolute error decreases from \\\\placeholder{X.X}\\\\,cm (step 500) to \\\\placeholder{X.X}\\\\,cm (step 30,000). We exclude OmniGuide from Table~\\\\ref{tab:main} because its evaluation uses RoboCasa rather than SafeLIBERO; conceptually, it represents the opposite end of the internalization spectrum (inference-time SDF reasoning vs.\\\\ our weight-level absorption).\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Inference latency.} \\\\ours{} in zero-overhead mode adds no latency. In optional verification mode (retaining the SDF head), overhead is ${\\\\sim}$5\\\\,ms. By contrast, AEGIS adds \\\\placeholder{XX}\\\\,ms and OmniGuide adds \\\\placeholder{XX}\\\\,ms per step.\\n\\n\\n\\n%=============================================================================\\n\\\\section{Conclusion}\\n\\\\label{sec:conclusion}\\n%=============================================================================\\n\\nThis paper presented \\\\ours{}, a training-time method that embeds geometric collision awareness directly into VLA flow-matching weights through SDF auxiliary supervision and a noise-aware collision-shaping loss. Unlike prior approaches that impose safety at inference time through guided sampling \\\\citep{song2026omniguide}, post-hoc trajectory correction \\\\citep{hu2025aegis}, or constrained reinforcement learning \\\\citep{zhang2025safevla}, \\\\ours{} produces a model whose standard forward pass generates inherently collision-aware actions with zero additional latency, memory, or access to scene geometry at deployment.\\n\\nOur results on SafeLIBERO demonstrate \\\\placeholder{XX}\\\\% collision avoidance rate (vs.\\\\ \\\\placeholder{XX}\\\\% for vanilla $\\\\pi_{0.5}$) while maintaining \\\\placeholder{XX}\\\\% task success, validating that SDF collision geometry can be internalized into VLA flow-matching weights through auxiliary supervision and a noise-aware collision-shaping loss, and that the resulting model runs through the unmodified $\\\\pi_{0.5}$ inference pipeline with zero overhead.\\n\\nThe ``train-with, infer-without'' paradigm is not specific to collision avoidance: the same framework could internalize other auxiliary objectives (energy efficiency, human comfort zones) by substituting the SDF head with a corresponding prediction target. Several directions remain open: neural SDF estimation from depth observations for real-world deployment, evaluation on additional VLA backbones (Octo, RT-2), and combining \\\\ours{}'s geometric shaping with constrained RL \\\\citep{zhao2024scpo,zhang2025safevla} for multi-step safety guarantees.\\n\\n\\\\subsection*{Limitations}\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Single benchmark and backbone.} All experiments were conducted on LIBERO/SafeLIBERO with $\\\\pi_{0.5}$. Validating on additional benchmarks (ManiSkill, RoboCasa) and VLA architectures (Octo, RT-2) would strengthen generality claims.\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Known geometry assumption.} Ground-truth SDF labels require analytical scene geometry (MuJoCo). Real-world deployment will need neural SDF estimation from depth or point clouds, and the effect of label noise on collision shaping remains uncharacterized.\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Simulation only.} All results are simulated; a real-robot validation is needed to confirm that internalized collision awareness survives the sim-to-real gap.\\n\\n\\\\subsection*{Broader Impact}\\n\\nA broader impact discussion is provided in Appendix~\\\\ref{app:broader_impact}.\\n\\n\\n\\\\bibliography{references}\\n\\\\bibliographystyle{plainnat}\\n\\n\\\\appendix\\n\\\\input{appendix}\\n\\n\\\\end{document}\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.5}}"

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"Existing approaches to VLA collision safety fall into two categories. Inference-time methods---OmniGuide \\\\citep{song2026omniguide} (SDF-guided denoising, ${\\\\sim}2\\\\times$ latency overhead), AEGIS/VLSA \\\\citep{hu2025aegis} (CBF trajectory projection), SafeDiffuser \\\\citep{xiao2025safediffuser} (CBF-embedded diffusion)---add computational overhead and risk pushing corrected trajectories into out-of-distribution states where subsequent predictions degrade. Training-time methods such as SafeVLA \\\\citep{zhang2025safevla} reduce violations through constrained RL but rely on abstract cost functions without geometric grounding. A fundamental tension persists: inference-time interventions add latency and risk OOD drift, while training-time RL methods lack geometric structure to reason about collision boundaries.\\n\\nWe draw on an observation from image generation: guidance distillation \\\\citep{meng2023distillation} has shown that inference-time guidance signals can be internalized into model weights during training. We transfer this principle to VLA safety, internalizing SDF collision geometry into flow-matching velocity fields for safe action generation. At low noise levels ($t$ near 0), the model's internal representations encode meaningful geometric structure; an SDF auxiliary head attached to these representations provides a differentiable collision proximity signal. By penalizing collision-prone predictions with a noise-dependent weight that activates only in the low-noise regime, we shape the learned velocity field to route around obstacles without corrupting the training signal at high noise levels.\\n\\nWe propose \\\\ours{}, a training-time method that embeds collision awareness into $\\\\pi_{0.5}$ through SDF auxiliary supervision and a noise-aware collision-shaping loss. A lightweight two-layer network predicts per-timestep minimum signed distances from the action expert's internal features. The collision-shaping loss penalizes trajectories violating a safety margin $\\\\delta$, weighted by $w(t) = \\\\sigma(\\\\alpha(t_0 - t))$ that activates only in the low-noise regime. Both SDF regression and collision penalty gradients propagate through the shared backbone, directly modifying the velocity field. At inference, the SDF head is discarded entirely: the model runs through the standard $\\\\pi_{0.5}$ pipeline with zero additional latency, memory, or access to scene geometry.\\n\\nOur contributions are as follows:\\n\\\\begin{itemize}\\n\\\\item We present the first method that internalizes SDF collision geometry into VLA flow-matching weights during training, producing a model that generates inherently collision-aware actions with zero inference overhead. The SDF auxiliary head is dropped at deployment, and the standard inference pipeline runs unchanged (Section~\\\\ref{sec:sdf_head}, \\\\ref{sec:inference}).\\n\\\\item We propose a noise-aware collision-shaping loss with a time-dependent weight function $w(t)$ that adapts to the flow-matching noise schedule, activating collision penalties only in the low-noise regime where the model's geometric predictions carry reliable spatial information. This prevents noisy gradients at high noise levels from destabilizing training (Section~\\\\ref{sec:loss}).\\n\\\\item We evaluate \\\\ours{} on SafeLIBERO (32 collision scenarios) and standard LIBERO, demonstrating \\\\placeholder{XX}\\\\% improvement in collision avoidance with negligible task performance degradation, validating a ``train-with, infer-without'' framework---analogous to guidance distillation in image generation---for embedding geometric safety into generative robot policies (Sections~\\\\ref{sec:experiments},~\\\\ref{sec:inference}).\\n\\\\end{itemize}\\n\\n\\n%=============================================================================\\n\\\\section{Related Work}\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Safe Robot Learning.}\\nInference-time VLA safety methods include SDF-guided denoising \\\\citep{song2026omniguide}, CBF-based trajectory projection \\\\citep{hu2025aegis}, and CBF-embedded diffusion \\\\citep{xiao2025safediffuser}. These approaches o\\n\\n... [截断: 输出过长，省略 18971 字符] ...\\n\\nar}\\n\\\\end{table}\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{No collision loss} ($\\\\lambda_{\\\\mathrm{col}} = 0$, $\\\\lambda_{\\\\mathrm{SDF}} = 0.1$). Retains SDF regression but removes the collision penalty, testing whether distance prediction alone suffices to improve safety.\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{No backbone gradient} (\\\\texttt{sdf\\\\_grad\\\\_to\\\\_backbone = False}). Detaches the SDF head's input from the computation graph so that SDF gradients train only the auxiliary head without modifying action generation weights.\\n\\n\\\\subsection{Analysis}\\n\\\\label{sec:analysis}\\n%-----------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Training dynamics.} The SDF head's mean absolute error decreases from \\\\placeholder{X.X}\\\\,cm (step 500) to \\\\placeholder{X.X}\\\\,cm (step 30,000). We exclude OmniGuide from Table~\\\\ref{tab:main} because its evaluation uses RoboCasa rather than SafeLIBERO; conceptually, it represents the opposite end of the internalization spectrum (inference-time SDF reasoning vs.\\\\ our weight-level absorption).\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Inference latency.} \\\\ours{} in zero-overhead mode adds no latency. In optional verification mode (retaining the SDF head), overhead is ${\\\\sim}$5\\\\,ms. By contrast, AEGIS adds \\\\placeholder{XX}\\\\,ms and OmniGuide adds \\\\placeholder{XX}\\\\,ms per step.\\n\\n\\n\\n%=============================================================================\\n\\\\section{Conclusion}\\n\\\\label{sec:conclusion}\\n%=============================================================================\\n\\nThis paper presented \\\\ours{}, a training-time method that embeds geometric collision awareness directly into VLA flow-matching weights through SDF auxiliary supervision and a noise-aware collision-shaping loss. Unlike prior approaches that impose safety at inference time through guided sampling \\\\citep{song2026omniguide}, post-hoc trajectory correction \\\\citep{hu2025aegis}, or constrained reinforcement learning \\\\citep{zhang2025safevla}, \\\\ours{} produces a model whose standard forward pass generates inherently collision-aware actions with zero additional latency, memory, or access to scene geometry at deployment.\\n\\nOur results on SafeLIBERO demonstrate \\\\placeholder{XX}\\\\% collision avoidance rate (vs.\\\\ \\\\placeholder{XX}\\\\% for vanilla $\\\\pi_{0.5}$) while maintaining \\\\placeholder{XX}\\\\% task success, validating that SDF collision geometry can be internalized into VLA flow-matching weights through auxiliary supervision and a noise-aware collision-shaping loss, and that the resulting model runs through the unmodified $\\\\pi_{0.5}$ inference pipeline with zero overhead.\\n\\nThe ``train-with, infer-without'' paradigm is not specific to collision avoidance: the same framework could internalize other auxiliary objectives (energy efficiency, human comfort zones) by substituting the SDF head with a corresponding prediction target. Several directions remain open: neural SDF estimation from depth observations for real-world deployment, evaluation on additional VLA backbones (Octo, RT-2), and combining \\\\ours{}'s geometric shaping with constrained RL \\\\citep{zhao2024scpo,zhang2025safevla} for multi-step safety guarantees.\\n\\n\\\\subsection*{Limitations}\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Single benchmark and backbone.} All experiments were conducted on LIBERO/SafeLIBERO with $\\\\pi_{0.5}$. Validating on additional benchmarks (ManiSkill, RoboCasa) and VLA architectures (Octo, RT-2) would strengthen generality claims.\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Known geometry assumption.} Ground-truth SDF labels require analytical scene geometry (MuJoCo). Real-world deployment will need neural SDF estimation from depth or point clouds, and the effect of label noise on collision shaping remains uncharacterized.\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Simulation only.} All results are simulated; a real-robot validation is needed to confirm that internalized collision awareness survives the sim-to-real gap.\\n\\n\\\\subsection*{Broader Impact}\\n\\nA broader impact discussion is provided in Appendix~\\\\ref{app:broader_impact}.\\n\\n\\n\\\\bibliography{references}\\n\\\\bibliographystyle{plainnat}\\n\\n\\\\appendix\\n\\\\input{appendix}\\n\\n\\\\end{document}\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.51}}"}

**Dimension 1: Logic flow between sections after compression**

- Abstract: Problem (VLA + collision) → Method (SDF head + collision-shaping loss + w(t)) → Results (SafeLIBERO placeholders) → Significance (train-with-infer-without). Clear and self-contained.
- Intro P1: VLA success → collision safety gap. 
- Intro P2: Two categories of approaches (inference-time / training-time) → tension.


- Intro P3: Guidance distillation insight → transfer to VLA safety → noise-aware mechanism.
- Intro P4: GeoSafe description (SDF head, collision loss, w(t), backbone gradient, zero overhead at inference).
- Contributions: 3 items (SDF internalization, noise-aware loss, SafeLIBERO eval).
- Related Work: 3 paragraphs (Safe Robot Learning, VLA Models, SDF + Guidance Distillation). Each positioned against GeoSafe.
- Method: Problem Formulation → SDF Head → Noise-Aware Loss → Training Pipeline → Inference. Logical progression that builds naturally from problem to solution.
- Experiments: Setup → Main Results → Ablation → Analysis. The analysis section has some structural issues though—the training dynamics paragraph mixes SDF head performance metrics with an explanation of why OmniGuide was excluded from comparisons, which feels disconnected. That baseline discussion probably belongs earlier in the setup section.
- Conclusion: Summary → Results → Future, with discussion woven into the third paragraph. The second paragraph does repeat some ground already covered in the opening, particularly around how the SDF geometry gets embedded into the model weights.
- Limitations: Three key constraints identified.

The intro's fourth paragraph is also doing too much heavy lifting—it goes into method specifics like the SDF head, collision-shaping loss, the weighting formula, and gradient propagation details. That's a lot of technical depth for an introduction when the Method section will cover it anyway.

Now I'm checking where the figures and tables are positioned and referenced throughout the document. Figure 1 is placed correctly after the Method introduction, and I'm tracking down the references for Figure 2 to make sure they're consistent. Now I'm verifying all the cross-references between the main document and appendix—checking that every reference in main.tex has a corresponding label in appendix.tex, and confirming there are no orphaned labels that aren't being cited anywhere.

I notice several appendix sections aren't being referenced from the main text: the implementation details, the SafeLibero comparison, full results, ablation studies, and compute requirements. At minimum, the full results and ablation sections should be pointed to from the Experiments section, and compute information could be referenced when discussing the training pipeline or analysis.

Now I'm checking whether the paper's main contributions are actually supported by the evidence presented in the methods and results sections.

For the noise-aware weight function w(t), there's a gap—it's claimed as a contribution but the ablation table doesn't include a specific row testing what happens with a constant weight instead of the time-dependent version. The ablation only shows the full model, removing the collision loss entirely, and removing backbone gradients, but the original ablation that would have tested constant w(t)=1 was removed earlier.

There's also a mismatch in the text: line 215 claims "four ablation experiments" but Table 2 only displays results for two ablations, suggesting the count wasn't updated when rows were trimmed. I should check if there are additional ablations documented in the appendix.

Looking at the appendix, I can see hyperparameter sensitivity studies for SDF loss weight, collision loss weight, collision threshold, and SDF label coverage—but these aren't referenced in the main paper's ablation section. The core issue is that C2's noise-aware weighting scheme lacks direct ablation support in the main paper since the constant w(t) comparison was removed from Table 2.

For the conclusion, most Discussion points made it in—scalability, SDF assumptions, generalization, and multi-step safety considerations—but the guidance distillation analogy that motivated the whole approach seems to have been dropped.

Now looking at Related Work, I need to check if the compression preserved the key positioning. The three sections cover safe robot learning (contrasting inference-time vs training-time approaches), VLA models (showing how GeoSafe extends geometric internalization), and SDF plus guidance distillation. The structure is there, but I should verify the distillation section actually completes its thought about how SDF relates to the broader guidance distillation framework.

One thing to flag: the GLaD citation mentions a specific 94.1% success rate on LIBERO that I can't independently verify without checking the original paper, so that's worth noting as a potential citation accuracy issue.

For language quality, I'm noticing em-dashes used for parenthetical insertions in a couple places—they're grammatically correct but worth being aware of as a stylistic choice that appears in the compressed version.

The bigger issue I'm catching is a clear discrepancy: the text claims "four ablation experiments" but only two ablation conditions are actually described in the results. I need to verify what the original four were supposed to be, since this is a factual inconsistency that needs resolving.

For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). {"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"62:We present \\\\ours{}, a training-time approach that embeds geometric safety awareness into a flow-matching VLA without incurring any additional cost at inference. A lightweight auxiliary head predicts SDF values from the model's internal representations, and a noise-aware collision-shaping loss penalizes trajectories entering collision regions. After training, the auxiliary head is discarded and the base $\\\\pi_{0.5}$ policy is deployed unchanged (Figure~\\\\ref{fig:overview}).\\n113:where $\\\\sigma$ is the sigmoid function, $t_0 = 0.5$ is the transition center, and $\\\\alpha = 10$ controls the sharpness. When $t \\\\ll t_0$ (low noise), $w(t) \\\\approx 1$ and the collision penalty is fully active. When $t \\\\gg t_0$ (high noise), $w(t) \\\\approx 0$ and the penalty is suppressed. The sigmoid provides a smooth transition that avoids discontinuities in the loss landscape (Figure~\\\\ref{fig:weight_curve}).\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 1.08}}"}
```

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"31:Existing approaches to VLA collision safety fall into two categories. Inference-time methods---OmniGuide \\\\citep{song2026omniguide} (SDF-guided denoising, ${\\\\sim}2\\\\times$ latency overhead), AEGIS/VLSA \\\\citep{hu2025aegis} (CBF trajectory projection), SafeDiffuser \\\\citep{xiao2025safediffuser} (CBF-embedded diffusion)---add computational overhead and risk pushing corrected trajectories into out-of-distribution states where subsequent predictions degrade. Training-time methods such as SafeVLA \\\\citep{zhang2025safevla} reduce violations through constrained RL but rely on abstract cost functions without geometric grounding. A fundamental tension persists: inference-time interventions add latency and risk OOD drift, while training-time RL methods lack geometric structure to reason about collision boundaries.\\n183:\\\\paragraph{Baselines.} We compare against two methods on the SafeLIBERO benchmark. (1)~$\\\\pi_{0.5}$ vanilla: the same base model trained with identical hyperparameters and data but without the SDF head or collision-shaping loss, serving as a controlled ablation of our method's contribution. (2)~AEGIS/VLSA \\\\citep{hu2025aegis}: the current SafeLIBERO state-of-the-art, which applies a plug-and-play Control Barrier Function (CBF) safety layer on top of a trained VLA. We exclude OmniGuide from Table~\\\\ref{tab:main} because its evaluation uses RoboCasa rather than SafeLIBERO; conceptually, it represents the opposite end of the internalization spectrum (inference-time SDF reasoning vs.\\\\ our weight-level absorption).\\n245:\\\\paragraph{Inference latency.} \\\\ours{} in zero-overhead mode adds no latency. In optional verification mode (retaining the SDF head), overhead is ${\\\\sim}$5\\\\,ms. By contrast, AEGIS adds \\\\placeholder{XX}\\\\,ms and OmniGuide adds \\\\placeholder{XX}\\\\,ms per step (Appendix~\\\\ref{app:compute}).\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.5}}"}
```
