{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"\\\\documentclass{article}\\n\\\\usepackage{corl_2026}\\n\\\\usepackage{amsmath,amssymb,graphicx,booktabs,xcolor,hyperref,microtype}\\n\\\\usepackage[normalem]{ulem}\\n\\n\\\\newcommand{\\\\placeholder}[1]{\\\\textcolor{red}{[#1]}}\\n\\\\newcommand{\\\\ours}{GeoSafe}\\n\\\\newcommand{\\\\pimodel}{$\\\\pi_{0.5}$}\\n\\n\\\\title{GeoSafe: Internalizing Collision Geometry into Flow-Matching VLA Weights for Zero-Overhead Safe Manipulation}\\n\\n\\\\author{Anonymous}\\n\\n\\\\begin{document}\\n\\\\maketitle\\n\\n\\\\begin{abstract}\\nVision-Language-Action (VLA) models have advanced robot manipulation toward general-purpose deployment, yet collision safety remains an unresolved challenge for real-world use. Most existing safety methods operate at inference time, adding computational overhead; training-time alternatives based on reinforcement learning reduce violations but lack geometric grounding for precise spatial collision reasoning.\\n\\nWe propose \\\\ours{}, a training-time approach that embeds collision awareness directly into flow-matching VLA weights. During fine-tuning, a lightweight auxiliary head predicts per-timestep Signed Distance Field (SDF) values from the model's internal representations, and a collision-shaping loss penalizes trajectories that violate a safety margin. A noise-aware weighting function $w(t)$ suppresses this penalty at high noise levels where geometric predictions are unreliable, activating it only in the low-noise regime where action predictions carry meaningful spatial structure. After training, the SDF head is discarded entirely: the model runs through the standard inference pipeline with zero additional latency, zero additional memory, and no access to scene geometry.\\n\\nWe evaluate \\\\ours{} on the SafeLIBERO benchmark (32 collision scenarios across 4 task suites), measuring Task Success Rate (TSR) and Collision Avoidance Rate (CAR). \\\\ours{} achieves \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% TSR and \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% CAR, compared to \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% TSR / \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% CAR for vanilla $\\\\pi_{0.5}$ and \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% TSR / \\\\placeholder{XX.X}\\\\% CAR for the strongest inference-time baseline. Our approach establishes a connection between guidance distillation in image generation and safety internalization in robot control, providing a ``train-with, infer-without'' framework for embedding geometric safety constraints into generative action policies.\\n\\\\end{abstract}\\n\\n%=============================================================================\\n\\\\section{Introduction}\\n%=============================================================================\\n\\nVision-Language-Action (VLA) models have emerged as a powerful paradigm for general-purpose robot manipulation. $\\\\pi_0$ \\\\citep{black2024pi0} demonstrated that a flow-matching architecture built on a pretrained vision-language model can master diverse manipulation tasks across multiple robot platforms, and its successor $\\\\pi_{0.5}$ \\\\citep{black2025pi05} extended this capability to open-world generalization through co-training on heterogeneous data. These models generate smooth, language-conditioned action trajectories by learning velocity fields that transport noise distributions to action distributions, enabling flexible deployment without task-specific engineering. Yet a critical gap separates laboratory success from real-world deployment: collision safety. When a robot executes a manipulation task in a cluttered environment, unintended contact with surrounding objects or the workspace boundary can damage both the robot and the environment, and create safety hazards for nearby humans. The problem is especially acute for VLA models, which generate entire action chunks in a single forward pass without explicit geometric reasoning about obstacle proximity.\\n\\nExisting approaches to VLA collision safety fall into two categories. Inference-time methods---OmniGuide \\\\citep{song2026omniguide} (SDF-guided denoising, ${\\\\sim}2\\\\times$ latency overhead), AEGIS/VLSA \\\\citep{hu2025aegis} (CBF trajectory projection), SafeDiffuser \\\\citep{xiao2025safediffuser} (CBF-embedded \\n\\n... [截断: 输出过长，省略 8377 字符] ...\\n\\n if $s(q_t) \\\\geq \\\\delta$ for all timesteps, where $\\\\delta > 0$ is a safety margin. Our goal is to train a policy that intrinsically generates trajectories satisfying this constraint, without requiring runtime collision checking or trajectory filtering.\\n\\n%-----------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n\\\\subsection{SDF Auxiliary Head}\\n\\\\label{sec:sdf_head}\\n%-----------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Architecture.} The SDF head is a two-layer multilayer perceptron (MLP) that maps the action expert's internal features to per-timestep signed distance predictions. In $\\\\pi_{0.5}$, the action expert is a Gemma-300M transformer that processes noisy action tokens alongside vision-language context. Let $f_t \\\\in \\\\mathbb{R}^{B \\\\times H \\\\times D}$ denote the action expert's output features (with $D = 1024$ for the Gemma-300M variant), which are normally projected to action-space dimension $D_a = 32$ by a linear output layer. We attach the SDF head to these features via a forward hook on the output projection layer, requiring no modification to the base model's source code. The head is defined as\\n\\\\begin{equation}\\n\\\\hat{s} = W_2 \\\\, \\\\mathrm{ReLU}(W_1 \\\\, f_t + b_1) + b_2,\\n\\\\end{equation}\\nwhere $W_1 \\\\in \\\\mathbb{R}^{256 \\\\times 1024}$, $W_2 \\\\in \\\\mathbb{R}^{1 \\\\times 256}$, and $\\\\hat{s} \\\\in \\\\mathbb{R}^{B \\\\times H}$ gives the predicted minimum signed distance for each timestep in the action chunk. The head contains approximately 263K parameters, which is negligible compared to the 300M-parameter action expert.\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Ground-truth SDF computation.} Per-timestep signed distances are precomputed offline using MuJoCo's \\\\texttt{mj\\\\_geomDistance}, measuring minimum distance between end-effector geometries and scene obstacles (excluding grasped objects). Labels are stored as compressed arrays and aligned to action chunks at training time. Implementation details are in Appendix~\\\\ref{app:sdf_labels}.\\n\\n%-----------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n\\\\subsection{Noise-Aware Collision-Shaping Loss}\\n\\\\label{sec:loss}\\n%-----------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n\\nA direct collision penalty on the SDF predictions would be problematic in the flow-matching setting: at high noise levels (large $t$), the noisy actions $x_t$ bear little resemblance to the clean trajectory, and the model's internal features primarily reflect noise rather than meaningful geometric structure. Penalizing collisions uniformly across all noise levels would inject noisy gradients that destabilize training. We address this with a noise-aware weighting scheme that activates the collision penalty only when the model operates on near-clean inputs.\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Noise-aware weight.} We define a time-dependent weight\\n\\\\begin{equation}\\nw(t) = \\\\sigma\\\\!\\\\left(\\\\alpha \\\\, (t_0 - t)\\\\right),\\n\\\\end{equation}\\nwhere $\\\\sigma$ is the sigmoid function, $t_0 = 0.5$ is the transition center, and $\\\\alpha = 10$ controls the sharpness. When $t \\\\ll t_0$ (low noise), $w(t) \\\\approx 1$ and the collision penalty is fully active. When $t \\\\gg t_0$ (high noise), $w(t) \\\\approx 0$ and the penalty is suppressed. The sigmoid provides a smooth transition that avoids discontinuities in the loss landscape (Figure~\\\\ref{fig:weight_curve}).\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{SDF regression loss.} The first auxiliary loss encourages the SDF head to accurately predict signed distances from the model's internal features:\\n\\\\begin{equation}\\n\\\\mathcal{L}_{\\\\mathrm{SDF}} = \\\\frac{1}{|\\\\mathcal{V}|} \\\\sum_{(i,j) \\\\in \\\\mathcal{V}} \\\\left(\\\\hat{s}_{i,j} - s_{i,j}\\\\right)^2,\\n\\\\end{equation}\\nwhere $\\\\mathcal{V}$ is the set of valid sample-timestep pairs with available ground-truth labels, $\\\\hat{s}_{i,j}$ is the predicted signed distance, and $s_{i,j}$ is the ground truth. This loss ensures the auxiliary head provides a faithful geometric probe of the model's representations, which in turn supplies meaningful gradients for the collision penalty.\\n\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 1.31}}"}

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"\\\\label{sec:setup}\\n%-----------------------------------------------------------------------------\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Base model and training.} We fine-tune $\\\\pi_{0.5}$ (openpi) with full parameter updates (not LoRA) on two NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 GPUs using DDP. The vanilla baseline uses the identical configuration without the SDF head or collision-shaping loss. Full training hyperparameters are in Appendix~\\\\ref{app:training}.\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{\\\\ours{} configuration.} The SDF head is a two-layer MLP (263K parameters) attached to the action expert via a forward hook. Loss weights are $\\\\lambda_{\\\\mathrm{SDF}} = 0.1$ and $\\\\lambda_{\\\\mathrm{col}} = 0.01$ with safety margin $\\\\delta = 2$\\\\,cm and noise-aware gating ($t_0 = 0.5$, $\\\\alpha = 10$). Full configuration is in Appendix~\\\\ref{app:geosafe_config}.\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{SDF ground-truth labels.} Per-timestep signed distance labels are precomputed offline from LIBERO demonstrations using MuJoCo's \\\\texttt{mj\\\\_geomDistance}, covering ${\\\\sim}$75\\\\% of all training tasks (details in Appendix~\\\\ref{app:sdf_labels}).\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Benchmarks.} We evaluate on two benchmarks. SafeLIBERO \\\\citep{hu2025aegis} consists of 32 collision scenarios across four suites that mirror the standard LIBERO \\\\citep{liu2023libero} task categories: SafeLIBERO-Spatial (4 tasks), SafeLIBERO-Object (4 tasks), SafeLIBERO-Goal (4 tasks), and SafeLIBERO-Long (4 tasks with longer horizons). Each task includes two difficulty levels (Level I and Level II), yielding 32 collision scenarios in total. We additionally evaluate on standard LIBERO-Spatial (10 tasks) to verify that collision awareness does not degrade manipulation performance on tasks without explicit collision hazards (details in Appendix~\\\\ref{app:safelibero}).\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Metrics.} We report three metrics. Task Success Rate (TSR) measures the fraction of episodes in which the robot completes the instructed task. Collision Avoidance Rate (CAR) measures the fraction of episodes free of any contact with designated obstacle objects. Safety Score (SS) is defined as $\\\\mathrm{SS} = \\\\mathrm{TSR} \\\\times \\\\mathrm{CAR}$ and captures the joint requirement that a policy must both complete tasks and avoid collisions. We target 3 random seeds per configuration; results in this version report seed=1 with 5 eval trials per task.\\n\\n\\\\paragraph{Baselines.} We compare against two methods on the SafeLIBERO benchmark. (1)~$\\\\pi_{0.5}$ vanilla: the same base model trained with identical hyperparameters and data but without the SDF head or collision-shaping loss, serving as a controlled ablation of our method's contribution. (2)~AEGIS/VLSA \\\\citep{hu2025aegis}: the current SafeLIBERO state-of-the-art, which applies a plug-and-play Control Barrier Function (CBF) safety layer on top of a trained VLA. <details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
All 5 fixes verified:

1. **Fig 1 reference**: ✅ Line 62 — `(Figure~\ref{fig:overview})` at end of Method intro
2. **Fig 2 reference**: ✅ Line 113 — `(Figure~\ref{fig:weight_curve})` in w(t) description  
3. **"four" → "two"**: ✅ Line 215 — "two ablation experiments" (grep for "four ablation" returns empty)
4. **OmniGuide moved**: ✅ Line 183 — in Baselines paragraph, removed from Training dynamics (line 243 now clean)


5. **Appendix refs added**: ✅ Lines 179, 237, and 245 now include proper cross-references to the appendix sections
6. **Conclusion P2 simplified**: ✅ Rewrote to be more concise with placeholders for the collision rate reduction and task success degradation metrics
7. **No new issues detected** — the document looks clean.

Now I'm shifting to evaluate this from a CoRL 2026 reviewer's perspective. The core contribution is embedding SDF collision geometry into the flow-matching VLA through an auxiliary head and collision-shaping loss, then dropping the head at inference for zero overhead. The novelty hinges on combining these elements in a new way and using noise-aware weighting for the collision loss during flow matching, though I'm noting that the individual components like SDFs and auxiliary heads are well-established in robotics.

The soundness assessment reveals a critical limitation: only one random seed with two ablations testing collision loss necessity and backbone gradient flow. CoRL reviewers typically expect at least three seeds with error bars, so this is a notable weakness despite the paper's transparency about it.

The experimental scope is quite narrow—a single benchmark, one VLA backbone, one simulator, no real robot validation, and just two baselines. While CoRL is more forgiving of simulation-only work than top ML venues, reviewers will likely want to see broader evaluation across different environments and models.

The most vulnerable aspect is the missing ablation on the noise-aware weighting function w(t), which is claimed as a contribution but never isolated experimentally, though sensitivity studies appear in the appendix.

Reviewers will probably attack the single seed (noise concerns), limited generalization evidence, absence of the w(t) ablation, and whether the ~20-50ms overhead of AEGIS is actually problematic for manipulation tasks. The paper's core strength—zero inference overhead without needing scene geometry at deployment—is genuine, but the practical significance of avoiding that overhead could be questioned since AEGIS requires runtime SDF computation anyway.

The safety gains look solid if CAR improves by 15-25 percentage points with minimal task success regression, though the evidence is confined to LIBERO tabletop tasks. On presentation, the 8-page structure is tight but works—the abstract is clear, intro flows well, related work is focused, method is mathematically sound, and experiments are organized across four subsections with supporting tables.

The main vulnerabilities are experimental scope (single seed, single benchmark, single backbone, only two baselines, no real robot validation), the risk that reviewers see this as incremental engineering rather than conceptual novelty, and the missing direct ablation on the noise-aware weighting scheme itself—the appendix covers lambda sensitivity but not the core w(t) contribution.

Looking at likely reviewer reactions, an optimistic read values the conceptual framework and zero-overhead appeal (weak accept territory), while a skeptical reviewer fixates on thin evidence and missing ablations (borderline reject). The paper probably lands in that borderline zone between weak accept and reject depending on who reviews it.

The real deciding factors are whether the "train-with, infer-without" framing feels genuinely novel or just incremental multi-task learning, whether one seed with two ablations passes the rigor bar for a top venue, and whether the safety gains are substantial enough to justify the limited experimental validation. Getting three seeds before the deadline would be a meaningful boost, and running the weight scheduling ablation even on a single seed would strengthen the case considerably.
```

Novelty — 中等偏弱 (5/10)\n\n**加分项：**\n- SDF 几何信息内化到 flow-matching VLA 权重是**新组合**——此前没有工作在 flow-matching 框架里做 training-time 碰撞安全\n- noise-aware w(t) 机制有技术针对性——直接回应 flow matching 中高噪声时空间信息不可靠的问题\n- \"train-with, infer-without\" 范式作为概念框架有一定普适性\n\n**审稿人可能的攻击：**\n- \"这本质上是 multi-task learning + auxiliary head，不是真正的 guidance distillation\"——guidance distillation 是把 classifier-free guidance 蒸馏进无条件模型，GeoSafe 并没有做推理时 guidance 再蒸馏的过程，类比是概念层面的而非机制层面的\n- GLaD (Guo et al., 2025) 已经做了几何信息内化到 VLA（spatial accuracy），GeoSafe 只是换了目标（collision → SDF），技术路径类似\n- 各组件（SDF、auxiliary head、sigmoid gating、forward hook）都是已有技术的组合\n\n**判断**：对于了解 distillation 文献的审稿人，novelty 论证会被挑战。对于 robotics-first 审稿人，\"zero overhead safety\" 的工程贡献更有说服力。\n\n---\n\n#### 2. Soundness — 弱 (4/10)\n\n这是论文最大的薄弱环节。\n\n**实验配置：**\n- **1 seed** — CoRL 通常期望 ≥3 seeds + error bars。论文诚实标注了 \"seed=1 with 5 eval trials per task\"，但审稿人会质疑结果是否 noise\n- **2 ablations** — No collision loss + No backbone gradient。这两个测试了最重要的设计选择，但覆盖不全\n- **2 baselines** — vanilla π₀.₅ + AEGIS。SafeVLA 缺席（代码未公开可以解释），但 baseline 数量偏少\n- **1 benchmark + 1 backbone** — 泛化性未验证\n\n**核心漏洞：C2 贡献 (noise-aware w(t)) 无 ablation**\n\nIntro 明确把 w(t) 列为第二大贡献，但 Table 2 没有 \"constant w(t)=1\" 对照行。Appendix 有 λ 参数敏感性分析，但**不是** w(t) 本身的消融。审稿人会直接问：\"How do you know the noise-aware weighting matters? Show me w(t)=1.\"\n\n**审稿人最可能攻击的点（按概率排序）：**\n1. \"1 seed 不够，无法判断 15-25pp 提升是真实的还是随机波动\"\n2. \"w(t) 是贡献但没有消融证据\"\n3. \"只有 LIBERO 一个 benchmark，generalization claim 不成立\"\n\n---\n\n#### 3. Significance — 中等 (6/10)\n\n**Zero overhead 的真实意义：**\n\n审稿人**会**说 \"AEGIS overhead is only ~20-50ms, negligible for manipulation at 5-10Hz\"。这个反驳是合理的——tabletop manipulation 不是 high-frequency control。\n\n但 GeoSafe 的真正意义不在 latency 而在**部署简单性**：\n- 不需要运行时场景几何（AEGIS/OmniGuide 需要 SDF 或 CBF 在线计算）\n- 不需要额外模块（直接用标准 π₀.₅ pipeline）\n- 不风险 OOD drift（不修改 inference 轨迹）\n\n论文目前强调 \"zero latency\" 但**低估了 \"no runtime geometry\" 的论证**。如果能在 §1/§5 更强调 \"部署不需要场景几何获取\" 这一点，significance 论证会更有力。\n\n**15-25pp CAR 提升** 在 SafeLIBERO 上是 substantial。如果 TSR 下降 ≤3pp，trade-off 很有吸引力。\n\n---\n\n#### 4. Presentation — 良好 (7/10)\n\n- 8 页紧凑但可读。Method 数学严谨，Experiments 结构清晰\n- Related Work 3 段 positioning 明确\n- Limitations 诚实（单 benchmark、已知几何、仅仿真）\n- 13 个 placeholder 待填——填数后 presentation 会进一步提升\n- Fig 1 overview + Fig 2 w(t) curve 两张图够用\n- 唯一担心：Table 1 只有 3 行（3 methods），视觉上略单薄。如果 page 允许，考虑加 per-suite TSR/CAR 拆分\n\n---\n\n#### 5. 薄弱点 Top 3（最可能被 reject 的原因）\n\n**#1. 实验不充分 (probability: 高)**\n1 seed + 2 ablation + 1 benchmark + 1 backbone + 2 baselines + no real robot。对于一个 claim \"internalizes collision geometry\" 的论文，这个实验规模不足以支撑结论的普适性。审稿人可能给出 \"interesting idea but premature for publication\" 的判断。\n\n**#2. Novelty 争议 (probability: 中)**\n\"Auxiliary head + loss + drop at inference\" 模式在 multi-task learning 中常见。guidance distillation 类比被挑战后，技术贡献缩小为 \"noise-aware w(t) + SDF collision loss on flow matching\"——审稿人可能认为 delta 太小。\n\n**#3. w(t) 贡献无实验支撑 (probability: 中)**\n作为 3 大贡献之一的 noise-aware w(t) 没有直接 ablation。审稿人会认为 \"you claim this is a contribution but don't validate it experimentally\"。这个漏洞叠加 #1 会加重 soundness 打分。\n\n---\n\n#### 预估分数\n\n| 审稿人类型 | 预估分 | 理由 |\n|-----------|--------|------|\n| Robotics 应用导向 | **Weak Accept** (5-6) | 认可 zero-overhead 工程价值，对 1 seed 容忍度较高 |\n| ML 方法论导向 | **Borderline Reject** (4) | 认为 auxiliary head + loss 不够 novel，实验单薄 |\n| Safety/Control 专家 | **Reject** (3-4) | 会质疑 \"只在 sim + 已知几何下验证，single-step safety 无保证\" |\n\n**Overall 预估：Borderline（介于 borderline reject 和 weak accept 之间）**\n\nAC 决策可能取决于：(a) 3 个审稿人的领域分布、(b) rebuttal 中能否补充 3-seed 结果和 w(t) ablation、(c) 是否有审稿人被 \"train-with, infer-without\" 范式说服。\n\n---\n\n#### 提升中稿概率的建议（按 ROI 排序）\n\n| 优先级 | 行动 | 预估提升 | 所需资源 |\n|--------|------|----------|----------|\n| **P0** | 跑满 3 seeds + error bars | 从 borderline → weak accept | 额外 4 runs × ~56h = 但可并行 |\n| **P0** | 补 w(t)=1 ablation（1 seed 即可） | 堵住 C2 贡献漏洞 | 1 run ~56h（可与 seed 2 并行） |\n| **P1** | 强化 \"no runtime geometry\" 论证（而非只讲 latency） | 加强 significance | 纯文字改动 |\n| **P2** | 如有 GPU 余量，跑 ManiSkill 或 RoboCasa 验证 | 回应 generalization 质疑 | 需要额外工程 |\n\n**关键节点**：如果截稿前能做到 3 seeds + w(t) ablation，中稿概率从 ~30% 提升到 ~45-50%。如果只有 1 seed，坦率说 CoRL 接收难度较大。",
  "project": "geosafe_vla"
}

Looking at the loss breakdown for geosafe, the collision rate metric is sitting at 0.630, which I should monitor as training continues. The GPU allocation matches the approved D005 configuration with baseline on GPUs 0-1 and geosafe on GPUs 2-3, so that's within the project's allowed range.

Training is running smoothly across both experiments—loss values are stable in the expected range, step times are consistent around 6.8-6.9 seconds, and GPU utilization is maxed out at 100% with normal temperatures. The 10K checkpoint will arrive in roughly 12-13 hours for baseline and about 13 hours for geosafe, putting completion around May 21 morning UTC.

Looking at the geosafe loss breakdown, the flow matching loss is slightly lower than baseline (0.0276 vs 0.0294), which could mean the SDF gradients are helping with training dynamics. The SDF regression loss is converging nicely at 0.0024, collision penalty is minimal at 0.0013, and that collision rate metric of 0.630 suggests a significant portion of training data involves collision-relevant states—worth noting for the early evaluation.

{
  "text": "## D005 训练进度审查 + Round 3 修复确认 + CoRL 中稿概率评估\n\n---\n\n### Part 1: D005 训练进度 [severity: info]\n\n4× GPU 全部 100% 利用率，训练正常。\n\n| 实验 | 步数 | Loss | Step Time | 10K ETA | 完成 ETA |\n|------|------|------|-----------|---------|----------|\n| baseline_v3_s1 | 3444/30K | 0.0294 | 6.83s | ~5/21 08:30 UTC | ~5/22 下午 |\n| geosafe_v3_s1 | 3120/30K | 0.0238 | 6.90s | ~5/21 09:30 UTC | ~5/22 晚间 |\n\n**GPU 边界合规**：baseline 在 GPU 0,1，geosafe 在 GPU 2,3，符合 D005 配置。✅\n\n**训练信号**：geosafe 分项 loss（fm=0.0276, sdf=0.0024, col=0.0013, cr=0.630）显示 SDF head 正在收敛，collision loss 很低表明大部分样本已在安全阈值之上。fm_loss 略低于 baseline 的纯 FM loss（0.0276 vs 0.0294），可能是正常波动，10K checkpoint eval 后可判断。\n\n---\n\n### Part 2: Round 3 修复确认 [severity: info]\n\n5 项修复全部核实通过，未引入新问题：\n\n| # | 修复项 | 验证方式 | 状态 |\n|---|--------|----------|------|\n| 1a | Fig 1 引用 | `grep -n \"ref{fig:\" main.tex` → L62 `(Figure~\\ref{fig:overview})` | ✅ |\n| 1b | Fig 2 引用 | 同上 → L113 `(Figure~\\ref{fig:weight_curve})` | ✅ |\n| 2a | \"four\" → \"two\" | `grep -n \"two ablation\" main.tex` → L215 | ✅ |\n| 2b | Appendix ablation 引用 | 正文 L237 新增 `Appendix~\\ref{app:ablation}` | ✅ |\n| 3 | OmniGuide 移至 Baselines | `grep -n \"OmniGuide\" main.tex` → 仅 L31(Intro), L183(Baselines), L245(Latency)，原 Training dynamics 段已清理 | ✅ |\n| 4a | SafeLIBERO 附录引用 | L179 `(details in Appendix~\\ref{app:safelibero})` | ✅ |\n| 4b | Compute 附录引用 | L245 `(Appendix~\\ref{app:compute})` | ✅ |\n| 5 | Conclusion P2 精简 | L256 改为 1 句 placeholder 数字句，无冗余复述 | ✅ |\n\n**附带确认**：\"four ablation\" 已无残留（grep 返回空）；\"Liu et al\" SafeVLA 引用不一致也无残留。论文结构审查 3 轮发现的所有问题均已修复。\n\n---\n\n### Part 3: CoRL 2026 中稿概率评估 [severity: warning]\n\n假设实验数据已填入，GeoSafe CAR 比 baseline 高 15-25pp，TSR 差距 ≤3pp。\n\n#### 1. Novelty（6/10）\n\n**优势**：\n- SDF internalization into flow-matching VLA 确实是新的——现有工作（AEGIS/CBF post-hoc, OmniGuide/inference-time SDF, SafeVLA/constrained RL）都没做过 training-time SDF → weight absorption\n- guidance distillation 类比从 image generation 迁移到 robot safety 有新意\n- noise-aware w(t) 设计是 flow-matching 专属的技术贡献\n\n**风险**：\n- 核心思路（auxiliary head + distill into backbone + drop at inference）在 VLA 领域已有先例：GLaD 做 3D feature distillation、OccVLA/SpatialVLA/GeoPredict 做 train-with-infer-without geometric supervision。审稿人可能认为 GeoSafe 是把同一 paradigm 换了个 target（collision→safety 而非 spatial awareness）\n- SDF head 本身是 2-layer MLP，architectural contribution 偏轻\n\n**审稿人可能说**：\"The train-with-infer-without idea is not new in VLA (GLaD, OccVLA). The main novelty is applying it to collision safety, which is incremental.\"\n\n#### 2. Soundness（5/10）\n\n**1 seed + 2 ablation 是最大弱点：**\n- CoRL 2026 审稿人几乎一定会问 \"Why only 1 seed? How do we know results aren't seed-dependent?\"\n- 只有 2 个 ablation（no collision loss + no backbone gradient），缺少 w(t) ablation（constant w(t)=1）——而 w(t) 是 C2 核心贡献\n- Appendix 有 sensitivity analysis（λ_SDF, λ_col, δ），但也是 placeholder，如果都是 1 seed 则统计力度不足\n\n**可能的攻击点**：\n1. \"Single seed results are unreliable for drawing conclusions about safety\"\n2. \"The noise-aware weight w(t) is a claimed contribution but has no ablation\"\n3. \"SafeLIBERO only — how does this generalize to other environments?\"\n4. \"Known geometry requirement limits real-world applicability\"\n\n**缓解**：如果 3 seed 实验能在 deadline 前跑完（GPU-hours 足够），问题 1 可化解。w(t) ablation 应恢复（即使放 appendix）。\n\n#### 3. Significance（6.5/10）\n\n**zero overhead 的价值确实存在但需要 nuance：**\n- 审稿人**会**说 \"AEGIS adds only XX ms per step, which is negligible for 5-10Hz control loops\"\n- 但 GeoSafe 的真正 significance 不只是 latency——是**不需要 runtime scene geometry access**。AEGIS/OmniGuide 都需要 SDF oracle 或 CBF 在 deployment 时可用，GeoSafe 不需要。这是 deployment simplicity 的优势，论文已在 Abstract 和 Method 中强调了\n- 单 benchmark (LIBERO/SafeLIBERO) + simulation only → significance 受限\n\n**审稿人可能说**：\"Zero overhead is nice but the practical significance is limited when (a) the inference-time overhead of alternatives is already small, and (b) all results are in simulation with known geometry.\"\n\n#### 4. Presentation（7.5/10）\n\n**8 页是足够的**：\n- 正文结构紧凑完整：Intro(4段+贡献) → Related Work(3段) → Method(5节) → Experiments(4节) → Conclusion\n- 5 页 appendix 补充了所有被移出的细节，cross-reference 完整\n- Fig 1（overview）和 Fig 2（w(t) curve）位置合理\n- 经过 3 轮审查，文字质量已无明显 AI 风格问题\n\n**小风险**：Table 1 和 Table 2 目前全是 placeholder——填数后如果数字不够好看，表格可能需要重新设计（例如加粗/下划线标注、调整列顺序）。\n\n#### 5. 薄弱点 Top 3（最可能被 reject 的原因）\n\n**#1 统计不充分（最高风险）**\n1 seed 在安全关键领域（safety-critical）尤其致命。如果 GeoSafe 与 baseline 的差距在 seed 间波动大，15-25pp CAR 提升可能不 robust。审稿人会质疑 \"Is this improvement real or just seed-dependent?\"\n→ **缓解**：3 seed 是必须的。如果时间不够，至少 2 seed + error bar。\n\n**#2 w(t) 贡献缺乏 ablation 验证**\nw(t) 是 Intro 三大贡献之一（C2），但 Table 2 没有 constant w(t)=1 对照。审稿人会说 \"You claim the noise-aware weight is a key contribution, but you don't ablate it.\"\n→ **缓解**：恢复 constant w(t)=1 ablation 行（可放 Table 2 或 Appendix），1 seed 即可。\n\n**#3 Generalization scope 窄**\n单 backbone（π₀.₅）、单 benchmark（SafeLIBERO）、simulation only、known geometry。Limitations 已诚实列出，但 4 个 limitations 叠加会让审稿人觉得 \"interesting idea but too early.\"\n→ **缓解**：Standard LIBERO 结果（已有）+ 如果有时间加 1 个额外 benchmark（ManiSkill）会显著提升。但时间可能不够。\n\n#### 预估评分\n\n| 审稿人类型 | 预估分数 | 理由 |\n|-----------|----------|------|\n| 偏 novelty 的审稿人 | **borderline** | \"Train-with-infer-without 不新，但 collision-specific 应用有价值\" |\n| 偏 rigor 的审稿人 | **weak reject** | \"1 seed + 缺 w(t) ablation 不够严谨\" |\n| 偏 practical 的审稿人 | **weak accept** | \"Zero overhead + no runtime geometry 有实际部署价值\" |\n\n**综合预估：borderline（偏 weak reject 一侧）**\n\n如果补上 3 seed + w(t) ablation → **borderline（偏 weak accept 一侧）**\n如果再加 1 个额外 benchmark → **weak accept**\n\n#### 给 Director 的建议优先级\n\n| 优先级 | 行动 | GPU-hours | 影响 |\n|--------|------|-----------|------|\n| P0 | 补跑 seed 2, 3（baseline + geosafe 各 2 轮，已有脚本） | ~200h（可在当前训练完成后并行） | 统计可信度从 \"不可接受\" → \"基本可接受\" |\n| P0 | 恢复 constant w(t)=1 ablation（1 seed 即可） | ~50h | C2 贡献有直接证据 |\n| P1 | 补全 appendix sensitivity analysis（已有表格，需填数） | 包含在 eval 中 | 增加 paper 厚度 |\n| P2 | 考虑 ManiSkill 迁移实验 | ~100h | Generalization 显著提升，但可能来不及 |\n\n**时间线评估**：CoRL 2026 paper deadline 5/29。D005 完成约 5/22 晚。剩余 ~7 天。补 seed 2+3 各需 ~50h（2 GPU），可在 5/22 后立即启动，5/25 前完成。w(t) ablation 也 ~50h。如果只有 4 GPU，需要串行 → 时间非常紧。**建议申请额外 GPU 实现并行**。",

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
The worker created an A3 ablation script for constant w(t)=1. Key observations:

1. **Implementation approach**: Setting `--sdf_noise_t0 100` so that w(t) = sigmoid(α(100-t)) ≈ 1.0 for all t ∈ [0,1]. Clever — no code changes needed. This is sound mathematically: sigmoid(10*(100-t)) = sigmoid(990 to 1000) ≈ 1.0 for all practical purposes.


2. **GPU assignment**: GPU 4,5 — but the server only has 4 GPUs (0-3). This is a critical boundary violation since westd-18751 has 4× NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell. The script would fail at launch, though it's possible this is a placeholder for after D005 finishes and GPUs are freed up.

Looking at the assignments more carefully: A1 gets GPU 0,1 and A2 gets GPU 2,3 — the same GPUs currently running D005. So A1/A2/A3 are clearly meant to run sequentially after D005 completes. But A3 requesting GPU 4,5 is physically impossible on this hardware. This needs a warning flag since the script would crash with a CUDA error if launched as written.

The fix is straightforward — A3 should use GPU 0,1 or 2,3 and run after D005 or another ablation finishes. I'm also double-checking the math on the constant weighting approach: setting t0=100 gives sigmoid(990) to sigmoid(1000) for t ∈ [0,1], both essentially 1.0, so that's a valid way to achieve constant weighting without modifying the code. The ablation design itself is sound too — A3 only toggles the noise-aware gating while keeping collision loss, SDF loss, and backbone gradient propagation identical to the full GeoSafe model.
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
This worker strengthened the "no runtime geometry" argument in 3 places (Abstract, Intro, Conclusion). Let me review:

1. **Abstract L20**: Added "(SDF fields, meshes, or depth maps)" — makes the claim more concrete. Good.

2. **Intro P2 L31**: Added a new sentence about inference-time methods requiring runtime geometry. "Critically, these methods require runtime access to scene geometry---signed distance fields, meshes, or depth maps---and online constraint solving, limiting deployment to environments with accurate geometric models." This is a good addition that strengthens the motivation. However, I need to verify: do ALL inference-time methods require runtime geometry? AEGIS uses CBF which needs a safety filter — does it need scene geometry? Let me think:
   - OmniGuide: Yes, uses SDF-guided denoising → needs SDF at inference
   - AEGIS/VLSA: Uses CBF trajectory projection → yes, needs a model of the environment for the CBF
   - SafeDiffuser: CBF-embedded diffusion → yes, needs CBF which requires geometry

So the claim is accurate for the three cited inference-time methods. ✓

3. **Conclusion L254**: Added "the model requires only the same RGB observations as the base VLA, eliminating the need for SDF fields, meshes, or depth maps." This is clear and correct. ✓

The worker also mentions 2 pre-existing `Missing $` errors at line 218 (experiment table area) — these are not from this change.

The edits effectively address the concern about dismissing the 5ms overhead by reframing the value proposition around deployment simplicity rather than latency alone. The repetition of "SDF fields, meshes, or depth maps" across Abstract, Intro, and Conclusion is consistent messaging, which is actually good practice even if a reviewer might notice it. The pre-existing `Missing $` error at line 218 should be flagged separately. Overall, these are solid improvements with no critical issues.
```
